Verifier hardening audit

Ten banking and payments coding tasks. Each is graded twice: by a naive grader written first, and by a hardened final grader. Every task also ships a reference solution, a structurally different valid alternative, and plausible wrong solutions.

How to review: scan the matrix for red naive “accepted ✗” badges sitting above green final “rejected ✓” badges, then select any cell to see its failing tests and pytest output.

Runs by task

Each run lists its failed tests first, then the tail of its pytest output.

01_idempotent_paymentsMake payment creation idempotent

A payment endpoint must charge a card once per Idempotency-Key, no matter how the client retries.

What the hardened grader checks

  • A sequential retry replays the original response without a second charge
  • Ten simultaneous duplicates produce exactly one charge
  • The same key used by two merchants creates two independent payments
  • Reusing a key with a different body is rejected, even while the first request is in flight
  • Declines are replayed, and replays survive an app restart
Ticket (PROBLEM.md)
# Ticket: payment retries must be idempotent

Our synthetic payment service already charges a declared gateway and returns a receipt.
Clients retry requests after losing a response. Implement the following observable contract.

Public interface: `app.main.create_app(database_url: str, gateway: Gateway) -> FastAPI`;
`app.gateway.Gateway.charge(merchant_id, amount_minor, currency, source_token) -> ChargeResult`.
`ChargeResult(charge_id: str, status: "succeeded"|"declined")` is a frozen dataclass.
The starter also exports `app.gateway.FakeGateway` for visible and naive smoke tests;
its `.calls` property is a tuple of charging calls. Hidden final tests inject their own Gateway.
`POST /v1/payments` takes `X-Merchant-Id`, `Idempotency-Key`, and JSON
`{amount_minor, currency, source_token}`. Money is positive integer minor units, never bool.
Currencies supported: CAD/USD/EUR/GBP (2 dp), JPY (0), KWD/BHD (3). No conversion occurs.

- Key required, length 1–255 characters; missing/empty/too long: HTTP 400
  `idempotency_key_required`. Merchant is a nonempty string; missing: 400 `merchant_required`.
- Invalid JSON body/fields: 422 `invalid_payment`. Ignore JSON member ordering. Body equality
  means equality of the three validated fields; no extra fields are accepted.
- Keys are scoped per merchant. Same merchant, key and body replay the original status and JSON
  exactly, without another gateway call. Declines (402 `card_declined`) also replay exactly.
- Same merchant and key with a different body: 422 `idempotency_key_reuse`, no charge,
  whether the first request is complete or in flight.
- Concurrent identical duplicates cause exactly one gateway charge. Each duplicate either gets
  the original response or HTTP 409 `idempotency_request_in_progress`. A later retry must replay.
- This behavior survives creating another app instance against the same database; no expiry.
- A successful receipt is HTTP 201 with `payment_id`, `charge_id`, `status` ("succeeded"),
  `amount_minor`, `currency`, and `merchant_id`. The payment id is nonempty and unique for distinct
  payments. Do not compare an id to any particular UUID format.
- Errors are JSON `{"error":{"code":"<snake_case>","message":"..."}}`; message text is free.
  No gateway exceptions or crash recovery are in scope. A charge returns normally with either status.

Preserve the factory and gateway protocol. Store state in the supplied PostgreSQL database;
the application may create its own schema. Tests inspect only the declared HTTP surface and
gateway calls. Timestamps in stored receipts use aware UTC; no timestamp field is required in JSON.
Run visible tests from starter: `python -m pytest tests -q` with `DATABASE_URL` set to a disposable
database. No customer data or real payment network is involved.
Reviewer notes (NOTES.md)
# Private draft — task 01 reviewer notes

The reservation implementation returns 409 during a charge; the advisory-lock implementation waits.
Both are valid under the public contract. Wrong variants isolate a realistic persistence,
namespace, or check-then-write defect; none change visible tests.

v2 note (see analysis/findings.md F-108): the starter now also lacks the request fingerprint and the
Idempotency-Key length validation (they moved into the reference), raising `ref_diff_lines` from 33 to 41.

## Regression rationale

`wrong_key_scope` is regressive by `check_realism`: the starter has no idempotency keys, so it passes
`test_same_key_different_merchants_independent` trivially. Rationale: The starter has no idempotency keys at all, so it is trivially merchant-independent; the natural first implementation uses the client-supplied key as the unique key, and scoping it by merchant is the extra step a hurried solver skips.

## Blind review
Author self-review, restricted to the ticket and starter (independent review still owed).
Ambiguities found: JSON equality vs member order, key length errors, merchant validation, receipt
fields, and failures during gateway calls. Resolved in PROBLEM.md: validated-field equality,
explicit status codes, free message text, and crash recovery excluded.
Three withheld-output predictions: missing key -> 400/idempotency_key_required; same key on
another merchant -> distinct 201 payment; completed decline retry -> identical 402 without charge.
No unresolved graded ambiguity after the ticket revision. This is not an independent model review.

## Flake measurement

Native Windows / PostgreSQL 16.13, 10 simultaneous callers, 150 ms gateway delay, 25 fresh-key rounds per test.
v2 re-measurement (starter changed, so the 20-repeat check was re-run; commit 1f1fa3e, clean tree):
60 final runs (20 per variant), 0 flips, all 60 verdicts ok. Wall time 149.441 s.

02_interest_accrualAccrue CAD interest at posting boundaries

Accrue daily interest on a CAD balance and post it at month ends, using Actual/365 and banker's rounding.

What the hardened grader checks

  • Half-cent ties round to even, not up
  • A 1,000,000,000.00 principal is exact to the cent
  • Small balances still accrue under daily compounding
  • Leap days and partial first and last months
  • Totals reconcile: final balance equals principal plus every posting
Ticket (PROBLEM.md)
# Ticket: accrue interest across partial calendar months

The synthetic CAD account needs correct daily accrual and explicit posting boundaries.
Keep `interest.accrual.accrue(principal: Decimal, annual_rate: Decimal, start: date, end: date,
method: Literal["simple_monthly","daily_compound"]) -> list[Posting]` and
`Posting(posting_date: date, interest: Decimal, balance: Decimal)` (frozen dataclass).

CAD has 2 decimal places. Principal is a nonnegative finite Decimal in whole cents; rate is
a nonnegative finite Decimal (a fraction, e.g. 0.05 means 5%). Invalid values/method or end before
start raise ValueError. These are dates, not timestamps; any datetime instead of date is invalid.

- Day count is Actual/365 Fixed, including leap years. Accrue for every date in `[start,end)`.
- Post on each month end within that period, and also on `end - 1 day` if it is not a month end.
  Return chronological postings. `start == end` returns `[]`.
- `simple_monthly`: interest at a posting equals current posted balance × rate × segment days / 365.
  Add each posting to the balance; thus it compounds at posting boundaries.
- `daily_compound`: each date, accrued += (posted balance + accrued) × rate / 365 with full
  intermediate precision. At posting, round accrued, add to balance, reset accrued to zero;
  discard the unrounded residual.
- Round only the posting to 0.01 using ROUND_HALF_EVEN. Intermediate arithmetic must use
  precision at least 28 or exact arithmetic. Both money fields must be Decimal with exponent -2,
  even when zero. Return the resulting balance after each posting.

Worked example: principal 1000.00, rate 0.0365 (so 0.0001 per day), start 2026-01-01, end 2026-03-01.
Both methods post on 2026-01-31 (31 days) and 2026-02-28 (28 days). January: 1000.00 x 0.0365 x 31 / 365 =
3.10, balance 1003.10. February: 1003.10 x 0.0365 x 28 / 365 = 2.8087..., posted as 2.81, balance 1005.91.
(With `daily_compound` the unrounded January interest is 3.1048...; both methods round to the same cents here.)

Only the declared interface is graded; internal representation and algorithms are unrestricted.
Run from starter: `python -m pytest tests -q`. All examples and accounts are synthetic.
Reviewer notes (NOTES.md)
# Private draft — interest reviewer notes

Reference accumulates daily in Decimal (34 digits), multiplying the simple segment in one
expression to preserve exact ties. Alternative uses closed-form Fraction compounding by segment.
The floating candidate is financially plausible and converts results back to Decimal; type
checking alone cannot reject it. Daily rounding is another common premature-normalization error.

## Regression rationale

`wrong_round_daily` is regressive by `check_realism`: the float starter passes its declared must_fail test `test_small_balance_daily_accrues`. Rationale: The float starter never rounds before posting, so it passes test_small_balance_daily_accrues; the Decimal rewrite that the ticket requires invites quantizing each day's accrual to cents to keep every money value at two decimals, which makes small balances accrue nothing.

## Blind review
Author self-review using only ticket and starter; independent review remains owed.
Ambiguities: negative rates/principal, initial cents, datetime vs date, rounding residuals, empty
periods. Resolved explicitly in PROBLEM.md.
Predictions: 50 CAD at 0.0365 for one day -> 0.00 interest; zero days -> []; 1000 CAD at 0.0365
for ten simple days -> 1.00 interest and 1001.00 balance. No unresolved graded ambiguity.

Self-review confirmation (2026-10-17): re-read only PROBLEM.md and starter files from disk; the three predictions above follow from the ticket. No additional graded ambiguity found. Independent review remains owed.

+def cents(value):

+    if 2 * remainder > value.denominator or (2 * remainder == value.denominator and quotient % 2):
+        quotient += 1
+    return quotient

03_fx_quotesQuote transfers with currency-specific minor units

Quote a currency transfer with the fee taken in the source currency and each currency's own decimal places.

What the hardened grader checks

  • JPY has 0 decimal places and KWD has 3
  • Cross rates via USD are multiplied without rounding the middle leg
  • The fee is deducted in the source currency before conversion
  • Exact half-unit ties round to even
  • Unknown pairs raise RateUnavailable
Ticket (PROBLEM.md)
# Ticket: quote a synthetic currency transfer

`fx.quote.quote_transfer(amount_minor:int,src:str,dst:str,rates:dict[tuple[str,str],Decimal],fee_bps:int)`
returns frozen `Quote(fee_minor:int,converted_minor:int,rate_used:Decimal,path:tuple[str,...])`.
Expose `RateUnavailable(ValueError)` for unavailable currencies/pairs.

Minor units: CAD/USD/EUR/GBP=2, JPY=0, KWD/BHD=3. Rates are positive finite Decimals in
destination major units per source major unit. All money values returned are int minor units.
Amount is nonnegative int, never bool; fee_bps integer in [0,10000]; invalid values -> ValueError.
Inputs use distinct src/dst; an unsupported currency -> RateUnavailable.

1. Fee in source minor units = amount_minor × fee_bps / 10000, rounded HALF_EVEN to integer.
   Deduct this fee BEFORE conversion. Returned fee_minor is this source fee.
2. Use direct `(src,dst)` rate if present, path `(src,dst)`. Otherwise require both `(src,"USD")`
   and `("USD",dst)`, multiply at full precision, path `(src,"USD",dst)`. Do not infer inverses or
   other routes. Missing required rates -> RateUnavailable. Direct rate takes precedence.
3. Convert net source minor units to source major units, apply the full rate, then scale to
   destination minor units. Round ONLY the final destination amount HALF_EVEN to integer.
   Do not round the USD leg of a cross quote. rate_used is the full direct/product Decimal.

Worked examples. (a) Cross quote: `rates = {("GBP","USD"): Decimal("1.25"), ("USD","CAD"): Decimal("1.40")}`,
`quote_transfer(20000, "GBP", "CAD", rates, 50)`: fee = 20000 x 50 / 10000 = 100 (1.00 GBP); net 19900 = 199.00 GBP;
rate_used = 1.25 x 1.40 = 1.7500; 199.00 x 1.7500 = 348.25 CAD, so `Quote(100, 34825, Decimal("1.7500"), ("GBP","USD","CAD"))`.
(b) Zero-decimal currency: `quote_transfer(5000, "CAD", "JPY", {("CAD","JPY"): Decimal("110")}, 0)`: 50.00 CAD x 110 = 5500 JPY,
so `Quote(0, 5500, Decimal("110"), ("CAD","JPY"))`.

Do not mutate rates. Zero amount returns zero amounts while still resolving the declared route.
No actual currency market or customer is represented. Run `python -m pytest tests -q` from starter.
Reviewer notes (NOTES.md)
# Private draft - FX reviewer notes

v2 starter (see analysis/findings.md F-110): the starter quotes direct pairs only, assumes two decimal
places for every currency, computes the fee but does not deduct it before conversion and has no cross
route through USD. Per-currency minor units, the cross rate at full precision, fee-before-conversion and
the precision rules are the ticket. The wrong variants are plausible completions of that starter:
`wrong_two_decimals` adds the cross route and fee deduction but keeps the starter's universal scale;
`wrong_round_cross_leg` rounds the USD leg of a cross quote to cents; `wrong_fee_after_conversion`
converts the gross amount and subtracts a destination-side fee. The exact rational alternative verifies
that the Decimal implementation does not define the answer by itself. Ordinary two-decimal direct
quotes can mask all three errors.

## Blind review
Author self-review: fee denomination, direct-rate precedence, unsupported currencies, inverse
rates, zero amount and acceptable fee range clarified in PROBLEM.md.
Predictions: JPY100 -> USD at0.01 ->100 cents; CAD3 at0.5 with5000bps ->fee2/converted0;
cross CAD1 via0.5 USD and1.5 EUR ->1 EUR cent. No unresolved graded ambiguity.
Independent review remains owed.

Self-review confirmation (2026-10-17): re-read only PROBLEM.md and starter files from disk; the three predictions above follow from the ticket. No additional graded ambiguity found. Independent review remains owed.

 __all__ = ["quote_transfer", "Quote", "RateUnavailable"]
 
 
+def nearest_even(value):
+    integer, residue = divmod(value.numerator, value.denominator)
+    doubled = residue * 2
+    return integer + int(doubled > value.denominator or (doubled == value.denominator and integer % 2))
+
+
 def quote_transfer(amount_minor, src, dst, rates, fee_bps):
     validate(amount_minor, src, dst, fee_bps)
-    try:
-        rate = rates[src, dst]
-    except KeyError as exc:
-        raise RateUnavailable((src, dst)) from exc
-    fee = int((Decimal(amount_minor) * fee_bps / 10000).quantize(Decimal(1), rounding=ROUND_HALF_EVEN))
-    converted = int((Decimal(amount_minor) * rate).quantize(Decimal(1), rounding=ROUND_HALF_EVEN))
-    return Quote(fee, converted, rate, (src, dst))
+    route = (src, dst) if (src, dst) in rates else (src, "USD", dst)
+    factors = []
+    for pair in zip(route, route[1:]):
+        if pair not in rates:
+            raise RateUnavailable(pair)
+        factors.append(rates[pair])
+    with localcontext() as arithmetic:
+        arithmetic.prec = max(34, sum(len(f.as_tuple().digits) for f in factors) + 10)
+        decimal_rate = Decimal(1)
+        for factor in factors:
+            decimal_rate *= factor
+    exact_rate = Fraction(1)
+    for factor in factors:
+        exact_rate *= Fraction(factor)
+    commission = nearest_even(Fraction(amount_minor * fee_bps, 10000))
+    destination = Fraction(amount_minor - commission, 10**UNITS[src]) * exact_rate * 10**UNITS[dst]
+    return Quote(commission, nearest_even(destination), decimal_rate, route)

04_ledger_reconciliationReconcile synthetic statements deterministically

Match bank statement lines to ledger entries with exact, split, refund and duplicate rules, deterministically.

What the hardened grader checks

  • A ledger entry is matched at most once, so duplicate lines stay unmatched
  • Split payments match only when all lines in the window sum exactly
  • Refunds match refund entries by original reference, never payments
  • Tie-breaks and the three-day window are applied in the stated order
  • Generated statements recover the known ground-truth matching
Ticket (PROBLEM.md)
# Ticket: reconcile a synthetic statement to ledger entries

Keep `recon.engine.reconcile(statement: list[StatementLine], ledger: list[LedgerEntry]) -> ReconResult`.
Public frozen dataclasses (also exported from recon.engine):
- `StatementLine(id: str, value_date: date, amount_minor: int, reference: str)`
- `LedgerEntry(id: str, booking_date: date, amount_minor: int, reference: str,
  kind: "payment"|"refund" = "payment", original_reference: str|None = None)`
- `Match(statement_ids: frozenset[str], ledger_ids: frozenset[str], rule: str)`
- `ReconResult(matches: list[Match], unmatched_statement: dict[str,str], unmatched_ledger: set[str])`

All amounts are integer minor units in one synthetic currency (CAD, 2 dp); statement amounts
are signed, ledger payment/refund amounts positive. Dates are calendar dates. IDs are unique
within each input, strings sort lexicographically. Inputs are valid and must not be mutated.
Apply complete passes in the following order; each id can appear in at most one match:

1. R1: process statements by `(value_date,id)`. Match an unmatched ledger **payment** with equal
   reference and equal signed amount, date difference <=3 calendar days. Choose smallest absolute
   date difference, then earliest booking_date, then smallest id.
2. R2: process remaining ledger payments by `(reference,booking_date,id)`. For each payment,
   collect **all** unmatched statement lines of the same reference within +/-3 days of that payment.
   If at least two lines and their signed sum equals its amount, match the whole group. Do not
   search subsets. Negative lines, if present, contribute their signed amount to this pass.
3. R3: process remaining negative statements by `(value_date,id)`. Match an unmatched ledger
   **refund**, with original_reference equal to the statement reference and positive amount equal
   to abs(statement amount). There is no date restriction in R3. Choose earliest booking_date,
   then smallest id. The refund's own reference need not equal the original reference.
4. R4: each remaining statement identical in `(value_date,amount_minor,reference)` to any matched
   statement gets reason `duplicate`; otherwise `no_match`. Unused ledger ids form unmatched_ledger.

Worked example (CAD minor units; every reference is a synthetic invoice id):

| Statement id | value_date | amount | reference |  | Ledger id | booking_date | amount | reference |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|---|---:|---|
| s1 | 2026-01-05 | 10000 | INV-1 |  | l1 | 2026-01-03 | 10000 | INV-1 |
| s2 | 2026-01-06 | 4000 | INV-2 |  | l2 | 2026-01-06 | 10000 | INV-2 |
| s3 | 2026-01-07 | 6000 | INV-2 |  |  |  |  |  |

`s1` matches `l1` by R1 (two days apart, inside the window). `s2` and `s3` are the only unmatched
lines for `INV-2` inside the window of `l2` and sum to 10000, so R2 matches both to `l2`.
Nothing is left unmatched.

Use rule strings `R1`, `R2`, `R3`. Match-list order is not graded. Exact membership, reasons,
partitions and no reuse are graded. Empty inputs produce empty collections.
Run from starter: `python -m pytest tests -q`.
Reviewer notes (NOTES.md)
# Private draft — reconciliation reviewer notes

Counts alone hide identity reuse. v2 starter (see analysis/findings.md F-0xx v2 redesign): the
starter implements R1 only, with a same-day window, no ledger consumption and a flat `no_match`
reason; the window, consumption, R2 split, R3 refund and R4 duplicate classification are the
ticket. The wrong variants are plausible completions of that starter: `wrong_no_consumption`
adds the window, R2, R3 and R4 but keeps the starter's non-consuming candidate lookup;
`wrong_split_ignored` adds everything except R2; `wrong_refund_abs` compares absolute amounts in
the exact rule. The alternative uses remaining-record maps and indexed references; the reference
extends the starter's candidate helpers and tracks consumed ids.

## Blind review
Author self-review on ticket plus starter. Ambiguities: R2 traversal with repeated references,
negative R2 amounts, R3 tie breaks/date window, ordering of result lists. Ticket now states all
of these. Predictions: exact amount at +3 days -> R1; duplicate exact statement -> one R1 and
one duplicate; parts 40+60 against 100 -> one R2 with both ids. No unresolved graded ambiguity.
Independent review remains owed.

Self-review confirmation (2026-10-17): re-read only PROBLEM.md and starter files from disk; the three predictions above follow from the ticket. No additional graded ambiguity found. Independent review remains owed.

05_double_entryEnforce an atomic double-entry journal

Run a double-entry journal where every post balances per currency and is all-or-nothing.

What the hardened grader checks

  • Debits equal credits in each currency; currencies cannot net against each other
  • A post with an unknown account leaves no lines behind
  • A transaction can be reversed once, and a reversal cannot be reversed
  • Every rejected operation leaves the trial balance unchanged
  • A state-machine test over random posts and reversals keeps every currency at zero
Ticket (PROBLEM.md)
# Ticket: enforce the journal invariant

`ledger.service.LedgerService(database_url)` operates a synthetic PostgreSQL ledger.
Expose frozen `Line(account_code: str, side: "debit"|"credit", amount_minor: int)` and:
`create_account(code,currency)`, `post(lines: list[Line],memo: str)->int`,
`reverse(txn_id:int,memo:str)->int`, `balance(account_code)->int`,
`trial_balance()->dict[str,tuple[int,int]]`.

Money uses integer minor units. Currencies: CAD/USD/EUR/GBP=2 dp, JPY=0, KWD/BHD=3;
no exchange or cross-currency netting. Errors derive from LedgerError:
UnbalancedTransaction, InvalidAmount, UnknownAccount, AlreadyReversed, CannotReverseReversal.

- A post has at least two lines (otherwise UnbalancedTransaction). Each amount is a positive
  int, never bool (otherwise InvalidAmount). Side must be debit or credit (LedgerError).
- All accounts must exist (UnknownAccount). For EACH currency independently, sum of debits
  equals sum of credits (otherwise UnbalancedTransaction).
- Posting is atomic. Every rejected operation leaves balances and trial balance unchanged,
  including a missing account after other valid lines. Inputs are not mutated.
- Balance is debits minus credits. trial_balance keys are all currencies of created accounts,
  values are `(total_debits,total_credits)` across all posted lines, including reversal lines.
  A currency without postings has `(0,0)`. balance of an unknown account raises UnknownAccount.
- reverse creates a new transaction, with every side swapped, links it to the original and
  returns its distinct id. An original can be reversed at most once (AlreadyReversed).
  A reversal itself cannot be reversed (CannotReverseReversal). An unknown txn raises LedgerError.
  Reversal counts as real debit/credit turnover; it does not erase original lines.
- State survives constructing a new service. Re-creating an account with the same code and
  currency is idempotent; different currency, empty code or unsupported currency -> LedgerError.
- Validation priority: line count, amount/side, accounts, balancing. On reversing a reversal,
  CannotReverseReversal takes priority. Memo is arbitrary synthetic text.

Worked example (CAD accounts `cash` and `revenue`, synthetic):
`post([Line("cash","debit",5000), Line("revenue","credit",5000)], "sale")` returns id 1;
afterwards `balance("cash") == 5000`, `balance("revenue") == -5000` and `trial_balance()["CAD"] == (5000, 5000)`.
`reverse(1, "undo sale")` returns a different id; both balances are then 0 and the CAD trial balance is
`(10000, 10000)`. Reversing id 1 again raises AlreadyReversed.

The service may create its own tables; tests observe only the declared methods. Run
`python -m pytest tests -q` from starter with DATABASE_URL pointing at a disposable database.
Reviewer notes (NOTES.md)
# Private draft - journal reviewer notes

v2 starter (see analysis/findings.md F-102): the starter posts lines on an autocommit connection
with no balance validation, no transaction wrapper and no reversal (no `reverse` method, no
reversal columns). Per-currency balance validation, atomic posting and the reversal lifecycle are
the ticket. The wrong variants are plausible completions of that starter: `wrong_cross_currency_sum`
validates balance but sums one grand total; `wrong_non_atomic` adds validation and reversal but keeps
the starter's autocommit connection and validates while inserting; `wrong_double_reverse` adds
reversal without the once-only check. The independent valid alternative checks a JSON recordset
using SQL aggregation and reverses by INSERT SELECT; it ignores the starter's schema module.

## Blind review
Author self-review from ticket and starter. Ambiguities: trial_balance key/value meaning,
invalid side/count errors, re-created accounts, unknown transaction errors, and reversal turnover.
All are specified in PROBLEM.md. Predictions: CAD debit100/USD credit100 -> UnbalancedTransaction;
unknown third account -> UnknownAccount with unchanged balances; reverse a reversal ->
CannotReverseReversal, no change. No unresolved graded ambiguity. Independent review remains owed.

Self-review confirmation (2026-10-17): re-read only PROBLEM.md and starter files from disk; the three predictions above follow from the ticket. No additional graded ambiguity found. Independent review remains owed.

+    def _validate(self, conn, lines):

+        totals = defaultdict(int)
+        for line in lines:
+            account = conn.execute("SELECT currency FROM accounts WHERE code=%s", (line.account_code,)).fetchone()

+    def _validate(self, conn, lines):
+        validate_lines(lines)
+        totals = defaultdict(int)
+        for line in lines:
+            account = conn.execute("SELECT currency FROM accounts WHERE code=%s", (line.account_code,)).fetchone()

06_available_balanceTrack active authorization holds and available balance

Compute available balance as posted funds minus active card holds, with capture, void and expiry.

What the hardened grader checks

  • Existing holds reduce what new authorizations can use
  • A partial capture releases the rest of the hold
  • Holds stop counting exactly seven days after creation
  • Approval is allowed exactly at the overdraft limit and declined one unit over
  • A random sequence of operations matches a simple model of the account
Ticket (PROBLEM.md)
# Ticket: available balance must include active holds

`holds.main.create_app(database_path,clock)->FastAPI` uses SQLite. The declared
`holds.clock.Clock` Protocol has `now()->datetime`, always aware (otherwise ValueError).
The factory seeds two synthetic accounts on first use, without resetting existing state:
`acct_standard`: posted10000, overdraft0; `acct_flex`: posted10000, overdraft5000.
All money is integer CAD minor units (2 dp). All amounts must be positive ints, never bool.

- GET /accounts/{id}/balance ->200 `{posted_minor,available_minor}`.
  available = posted minus sum of active, unexpired holds.
- POST /accounts/{id}/authorizations `{amount_minor}` ->201 `{auth_id}` iff amount <=
  available + that account's overdraft limit; otherwise402 `insufficient_funds` with no effect.
- A hold expires exactly 7×24 hours after creation, and stops counting at that instant.
- POST /authorizations/{id}/capture `{amount_minor}`: amount <= held amount; overcapture ->422
  `capture_exceeds_hold` with no effect. Successful capture ->200 `{auth_id,status:"captured"}`,
  deduct amount from posted balance, close the hold and release ALL unused remainder.
- POST /authorizations/{id}/void ->200 `{auth_id,status:"voided"}`, close hold, no posted change.
- Capturing/voiding an expired or already closed hold ->409 `authorization_closed`, no effect.
- Invalid body/amount ->422 `invalid_amount`; unknown account/auth ->404 `not_found`.
  Errors are `{"error":{"code":"...","message":"..."}}`; message text is free.
  Validate amount before closed status for capture. Void has no JSON body.
- Auth ids are nonempty unique strings; available may be negative within overdraft.
  State survives creating another app on the same file. Operations are atomic and per account.

Worked example (`acct_standard`, CAD minor units): authorize 3000 -> 201 and the balance is
`{posted_minor: 10000, available_minor: 7000}`. Capture 1200 -> 200 and the balance is
`{posted_minor: 8800, available_minor: 8800}`: the 1200 is deducted from posted and the unused 1800 of the
hold is released. A further authorization of 8801 is declined with 402 `insufficient_funds`; 8800 is approved.

Tests use the HTTP interface and the injected clock only. No real accounts, system clock delays
or network payments. Run `python -m pytest tests -q` from starter.
Reviewer notes (NOTES.md)
# Private draft - holds reviewer notes

v2 starter (see analysis/findings.md F-103): authorizations are recorded and approved against posted
funds plus overdraft, and `available_minor` always equals `posted_minor`; there is no holds model, no
capture or void endpoint and no expiry. The wrong variants are plausible completions of that starter:
`wrong_available_ignores_holds` adds capture, void and expiry but keeps the starter's availability;
`wrong_partial_capture_keeps_remainder` adds holds but leaves the unused part of a partially captured
hold active; `wrong_expired_holds_count` adds holds and rejects expired capture/void but still counts
expired active records against availability. The alternative retires expired records at transaction
entry and recomputes availability from the remaining active records.

## Blind review
Author self-review clarified account seeding, exact expiry instant, partial-capture response,
closed/overcapture error priority, persistence and amount validation in the ticket.
Predictions: reserve1000/capture400 ->posted9600/available9600; at7days hold stops counting;
flex15000 authorized then1 declined. No unresolved graded ambiguity. Independent review owed.

Self-review confirmation (2026-10-17): re-read only PROBLEM.md and starter files from disk; the three predictions above follow from the ticket. No additional graded ambiguity found. Independent review remains owed.

+                return error(404, "not_found")
+            if auth["state"] != "active" or instant(clock) >= auth["created"] + HOLD_SECONDS:
+                return error(409, "authorization_closed")
+            if capture is not None:
+                if capture > auth["amount"]:
+                    return error(422, "capture_exceeds_hold")
+                db.execute("UPDATE accounts SET posted=posted-? WHERE id=?", (capture, auth["account"]))
+            state = "captured" if capture is not None else "voided"
+            db.execute("UPDATE authorizations SET state=? WHERE id=?", (state, id))
+            return {"auth_id": id, "status": state}
+
+    @api.post("/authorizations/{id}/capture")
+    async def capture(id: str, request: Request):
+        value = await amount(request)
+        return error(422, "invalid_amount") if value is None else close(id, value)
+
+    @api.post("/authorizations/{id}/void")
+    def void(id: str):
+        return close(id)
     return api

+++ reference/holds\storage.py
@@ -24,6 +24,15 @@

+    @contextmanager
+    def current_state():
+        with transaction(database_path) as connection:
+            connection.execute("UPDATE authorizations SET state='expired' WHERE state='active' AND created<=?",
+                               [instant(clock) - 604800])
+            yield connection
+
+    async def number(request):
+        try:
+            payload = await request.json()
+            if isinstance(payload, dict) and set(payload) == {"amount_minor"}:
+                n = payload["amount_minor"]
+                if type(n) is int and n > 0:
+                    return n
+        except ValueError:
+            pass
+        return None
+
+    def funds(db, id):
+        result = db.execute("SELECT posted,overdraft FROM accounts WHERE id=?", [id]).fetchone()
+        if result is None:
+            return None
+        total, = db.execute("SELECT COALESCE(SUM(amount),0) FROM authorizations WHERE account=? AND state='active'", [id]).fetchone()
+        return result[0], result[0] - total, result[1]
+
+    @application.get("/accounts/{id}/balance")
+    def get_balance(id: str):
+        with current_state() as db:
+            totals = funds(db, id)
+            return failure(404, "not_found") if totals is None else {"posted_minor": totals[0], "available_minor": totals[1]}
+
+    @application.post("/accounts/{id}/authorizations")
+    async def reserve(id: str, request: Request):
+        n = await number(request)
+        if n is None:
+            return failure(422, "invalid_amount")
+        with current_state() as db:
+            totals = funds(db, id)
+            if totals is None:
+                return failure(404, "not_found")
+            if n > totals[1] + totals[2]:
+                return failure(402, "insufficient_funds")
+            key = str(uuid4())
+            db.execute("INSERT INTO authorizations VALUES (?,?,?,?,'active')", [key, id, n, instant(clock)])
+            return JSONResponse({"auth_id": key}, status_code=201)
+
+    def mutate(key, action, n=0):
+        with current_state() as db:
+            record = db.execute("SELECT account,amount,state FROM authorizations WHERE id=?", [key]).fetchone()
+            if record is None:
+                return failure(404, "not_found")
+            owner, reserved, status = record
+            if status != "active":
+                return failure(409, "authorization_closed")
+            if action == "captured":
+                if n > reserved:
+                    return failure(422, "capture_exceeds_hold")
+                db.execute("UPDATE accounts SET posted=posted-? WHERE id=?", [n, owner])
+            db.execute("UPDATE authorizations SET state=? WHERE id=?", [action, key])
+            return {"auth_id": key, "status": action}
+
+    @application.post("/authorizations/{id}/capture")
+    async def settle(id: str, request: Request):
+        n = await number(request)
+        return failure(422, "invalid_amount") if n is None else mutate(id, "captured", n)
+
+    @application.post("/authorizations/{id}/void")
+    def release(id: str):
+        return mutate(id, "voided")

07_statement_periodsSelect transactions by the account calendar month

Select a month's transactions using midnight in the account's own time zone, converted to UTC.

What the hardened grader checks

  • Local midnight boundaries for Toronto and Kolkata
  • Months that contain a daylight-saving change, including Auckland
  • A transaction at 23:59:59.5 local time still belongs to the month
  • Thirteen consecutive months select every transaction exactly once
  • Naive timestamps, unknown zones and invalid months raise ValueError
Ticket (PROBLEM.md)
# Ticket: monthly statements follow the account timezone

`statements.period.period_bounds(account_tz:str,year:int,month:int)->tuple[datetime,datetime]`
returns aware UTC instants for a half-open interval. `select(txns:list[Txn],account_tz,year,month)`
returns transaction ids sorted by instant, then lexicographic id. Frozen `Txn(id:str,at:datetime)`
is public. IDs are unique and timestamps may have arbitrary aware offsets and microseconds.

The period is the calendar month in the account's IANA timezone: local 00:00 on day 1 inclusive
through local 00:00 on day 1 of the next month exclusive, converted to UTC using zoneinfo.
An unknown time zone name, a non-int (or bool) year or month, a month outside 1-12 or a year outside
1900-2098 raises ValueError from both functions. Daylight saving rules apply independently to both
endpoints. Each input timestamp must be aware with a non-None utcoffset,
otherwise select raises ValueError, even when that transaction would be outside the month.
Do not mutate inputs. Empty input returns []. Money is not part of this interface.

Worked example: for an `America/Toronto` account, January 2026 is the interval
`[2026-01-01T05:00:00Z, 2026-02-01T05:00:00Z)` (Toronto is UTC-5 in January). A transaction at
`2026-02-01T04:30:00Z` is 23:30 on 31 January in Toronto, so it belongs to January; one at
`2026-02-01T05:30:00Z` is 00:30 on 1 February and belongs to February. So selecting January 2026 for
`Txn("a", 2026-02-01T04:30Z)` and `Txn("b", 2026-02-01T05:30Z)` returns `["a"]`.

Run from starter: `python -m pytest tests -q`. The records are entirely synthetic.
Reviewer notes (NOTES.md)
# Private draft - period reviewer notes

v2 starter (see analysis/findings.md F-106): the starter derives every account's month from UTC
(`account_tz` is ignored), sorts equal instants by input order only and has no time-zone, month or
year validation. Time-zone months, the id tie-break and the validation are the ticket. The wrong
variants are plausible completions of that starter: `wrong_utc_month` adds the validation and
tie-break but keeps UTC month boundaries; `wrong_inclusive_2359` uses the zone but ends the period
one second before local midnight; `wrong_fixed_offset` uses the zone's standard-time offset all year.
The alternative tests local calendar membership for each timestamp, so a grader requiring the
selector to call period_bounds would be invalid.

## Regression rationale

`wrong_inclusive_2359` is regressive by `check_realism` (the v2 starter passes its declared must_fail test `test_microsecond_boundary`, because the UTC starter already uses an exclusive next-month end). Rationale: The UTC starter already ends the period at the exclusive start of the next month, so test_microsecond_boundary passes on it; moving the period to local time invites rewriting the end as the last second of the local month (23:59:59), the way monthly statements are usually described in prose, which drops the final fractional second.

## Blind review
Author self-review: ordering of equal instants, microsecond precision, invalid timestamps outside
the window and year limits clarified in the ticket. Predictions: Toronto March 2026 starts05:00Z
and ends04:00Z; at end exactly -> excluded; last microsecond before end -> included.
No unresolved graded ambiguity. Independent review remains owed.

Self-review confirmation (2026-10-17): re-read only PROBLEM.md and starter files from disk; the three predictions above follow from the ticket. No additional graded ambiguity found. Independent review remains owed.

08_refund_limitsLimit refunds to the captured amount

Allow partial refunds up to the captured amount, which may be less than what was authorized.

What the hardened grader checks

  • Partial refunds add up and cannot exceed the capture
  • The ceiling is the captured amount, not the authorized amount
  • A rejected refund does not use up the remaining budget
  • Uncaptured and unknown payments are refused
  • Random refund sequences match a simple remaining-budget model
Ticket (PROBLEM.md)
# Ticket: refund only captured funds

`refunds.service.RefundService(db_path)` uses SQLite and exposes `create_payment(authorized_minor)->id`,
`capture(id,amount_minor)`, `refund(id,amount_minor)->refund_id`, `refunded_total(id)->int`.
Exceptions: RefundExceedsCaptured, PaymentNotCaptured, InvalidAmount (all derive from ValueError).

All money is integer minor units in one currency (CAD, 2 dp), never bool. Authorization, capture
and refund amounts must be positive ints, otherwise InvalidAmount. An unknown payment id raises ValueError from capture, refund and refunded_total (after the amount has
been validated).
Each payment may be captured once, at most its authorization; invalid/repeated capture -> InvalidAmount.
Refund before capture -> PaymentNotCaptured. Partial refunds are allowed. The cumulative successful
refund amount may not exceed the captured amount, which may be less than authorized. Exceeding
remaining captured funds -> RefundExceedsCaptured. Rejected attempts must never count toward
refunded_total or reduce the future budget. Validate positive amount before capture status.

Refund ids are distinct positive ints. Capture and successful refunds survive a new service instance
on the same file. Operations are atomic; no rejected operation may alter successful totals.
Worked example: `create_payment(10000)`, `capture(id, 6000)`. `refund(id, 4000)` succeeds and
`refunded_total(id) == 4000`. `refund(id, 2500)` then raises RefundExceedsCaptured, because only 2000 of the
captured 6000 remains, and `refunded_total(id)` is still 4000.

No settlement network or real customer is involved. Preserve the interface and run
`python -m pytest tests -q` from starter.
Reviewer notes (NOTES.md)
# Private draft - refund reviewer notes

v2 starter (see analysis/findings.md F-107): the starter records any refund of a captured payment
with no upper limit, lets a payment be captured repeatedly, runs without an explicit write
transaction and fails with an AttributeError on an unknown payment id. The cumulative cap on captured
funds, capture-once, atomic write transactions and the unknown-id rule are the ticket. The wrong
variants are plausible completions of that starter: `wrong_checks_single_refund` caps each refund at
the captured amount but not the running total; `wrong_uses_authorized` caps the running total at the
authorized amount; `wrong_counts_rejected` caps correctly but adds a rejected attempt to the
refunded counter before raising. The correct counter-based service and the append-only event
alternative share only the public errors and validation.

## Blind review
Author self-review: repeated capture, bool/zero, capture before refund and validation priority
were unspecified in the starter. The ticket now resolves them. Predictions: auth100/capture60/
refund40 then30 -> first accepted, second RefundExceedsCaptured; reject101 then refund100 ->
accepted100; uncaptured refund1 -> PaymentNotCaptured. No unresolved graded ambiguity.
Independent review remains owed.

Self-review confirmation (2026-10-17): re-read only PROBLEM.md and starter files from disk; the three predictions above follow from the ticket. No additional graded ambiguity found. Independent review remains owed.

+@contextmanager
+def journal(filename):
+    connection = sqlite3.connect(filename)
+    try:
+        with connection:
+            connection.execute("BEGIN IMMEDIATE")
+            yield connection
+    finally:
+        connection.close()
+
+
+def append(connection, tag, owner, payload):
+    inserted = connection.execute("INSERT INTO event_log(tag,owner,payload) VALUES (?,?,?)",
+                                  [tag, owner, json.dumps(payload)])
+    return inserted.lastrowid
+
+
+def known_state(connection, number):
+    state = reconstruct(connection, number)
+    if not state["known"]:
+        raise ValueError(f"unknown payment {number!r}")
+    return state
+
+
+def reconstruct(connection, number):
+    state = {"known": False, "ceiling": 0, "settled": None, "returned": 0}
+    history = connection.execute("SELECT tag,payload FROM event_log WHERE number=? OR owner=? ORDER BY number",
+                                 [number, number])
+    for tag, payload in history:
+        value = json.loads(payload)["minor"]
+        if tag == "approval":
+            state["known"] = True
+            state["ceiling"] = value
+        elif tag == "settlement":
+            state["settled"] = value
+        elif tag == "credit":
+            state["returned"] += value
+    return state
+
+

09_tiered_feesApply a non-marginal tiered fee schedule

Price a payment at one tier selected by an inclusive upper bound, then round and clamp the fee.

What the hardened grader checks

  • Amounts exactly on a tier bound use that tier
  • The whole amount is priced at one rate, not band by band
  • Half-unit results round up, using inputs found by search
  • The fee is clamped to the schedule's minimum and maximum
  • Invalid schedules are rejected for any amount
Ticket (PROBLEM.md)
# Ticket: price the whole payment at its selected tier

`fees.schedule.compute_fee(amount_minor:int,schedule:Schedule)->int` returns integer minor units.
Public frozen dataclasses: `Tier(up_to_minor:int|None,bps:int,fixed_minor:int)` and
`Schedule(tiers:list[Tier],min_fee_minor:int,max_fee_minor:int)`.

Amounts are nonnegative ints, never bool; negative/noninteger -> ValueError. Schedule tiers are
ordered by strictly increasing nonnegative upper bounds, ending with exactly one None (infinity).
Bps/fixed/min/max are nonnegative ints, min <= max. `compute_fee` raises ValueError for an invalid
schedule (checked on every call, whatever the amount).

- Select the FIRST tier where amount <= up_to_minor, or None. Upper bounds are INCLUSIVE.
- Pricing is non-marginal: apply that selected tier's bps to the ENTIRE amount, then add its
  fixed fee. Do not sum bands.
- Raw fee = amount × bps / 10000 + fixed_minor. Round once to an integer using ROUND_HALF_UP,
  then clamp to [min_fee_minor,max_fee_minor]. This deliberately differs from interest's half-even.
- Zero amounts still incur the selected fixed fee, subject to the clamp. Input schedules are not
  mutated. The currency is implicit; all numbers use the same minor unit (e.g. CAD has 2 dp).

Worked example: tiers `[Tier(10000, 100, 10), Tier(20000, 50, 20), Tier(None, 25, 30)]` with
`min_fee_minor=25`, `max_fee_minor=100`. An amount of 5000 uses the first tier: 5000 x 100 bps = 50, plus 10, so 60.
An amount of 15000 uses the second tier on the whole amount: 75 + 20 = 95. An amount of 100 would cost 11
and is raised to the minimum, 25. An amount of 30000 would cost 75 + 30 = 105 and is capped at 100.

Run from starter: `python -m pytest tests -q`. No real fees, networks or institutions are represented.
Reviewer notes (NOTES.md)
# Private draft - fee reviewer notes

v2 starter (see analysis/findings.md F-104): `compute_fee` prices every amount with the first tier's
rate using truncating integer division and no clamp, no amount validation and no schedule validation.
Tier selection, half-up rounding, the clamp and the validation are the ticket. The wrong variants are
plausible completions of that starter: `wrong_exclusive_boundary` selects tiers with `<`,
`wrong_float_bps` rounds half up using a float percentage, `wrong_marginal` prices bands separately.
Integer numerator rounding is independent of the alternative's Decimal quantization. Float
counterexamples are discovered by tools/find_ties.py, not chosen to fit a test.

## Blind review
Author self-review: upper-bound inclusivity, zero, fixed-fee clamp order and malformed schedules
were ambiguous in the starter; all are explicit in the ticket. Predictions: amount5000/bps3 ->2;
amount10000 uses the first inclusive tier; zero with fixed5/min10 ->10.
No unresolved graded ambiguity. Independent review remains owed.

Self-review confirmation (2026-10-17): re-read only PROBLEM.md and starter files from disk; the three predictions above follow from the ticket. No additional graded ambiguity found. Independent review remains owed.

+    if not bounds or bounds[-1] is not None or None in bounds[:-1]:
+        raise ValueError("bounded tiers then one unbounded tier required")
+    bounded = bounds[:-1]

+        raise ValueError("tier bounds must strictly increase")

+        return schedule.min_fee_minor
+    if fee > schedule.max_fee_minor:
+        return schedule.max_fee_minor

10_batch_parserValidate an entire SBF-80 payment batch

Validate an invented 80-character fixed-width payment file and reject the whole batch on any error.

What the hardened grader checks

  • CRLF and LF files parse identically
  • The trailer's count, control total and account hash total are all checked
  • One bad detail line rejects the entire batch
  • Every error is reported with its line number
  • Rendering and re-parsing a random valid batch round-trips; one changed digit is caught
Ticket (PROBLEM.md)
# Ticket: validate invented SBF-80 batch files atomically

`batch.parser.parse(data:bytes)->BatchResult` returns frozen dataclasses exported from that module:
`Payment(payee_account:str,amount_minor:int,reference:str)`, `BatchError(line_no:int,code:str)`,
`BatchResult(accepted:bool,payments:list[Payment],errors:list[BatchError])`.

SBF-80 is invented for this synthetic task. Each record is exactly 80 printable ASCII characters,
followed by LF or CRLF. A final terminator is optional; mixed LF/CRLF is accepted. Strip the
terminator before checking width and slicing. Interior blank lines are records and invalid.
Bare CR, non-ASCII or control bytes are invalid_encoding. All positions below are 1-based inclusive.

| Record | Columns | Field |
|---|---|---|
| H | 1 | H |
| H | 2–13 | batch id: 12 uppercase ASCII letters/digits |
| H | 14–21 | creation date YYYYMMDD, valid calendar date |
| H | 22–24 | currency CAD, USD, EUR, GBP, JPY, KWD or BHD |
| H | 25–80 | spaces |
| D | 1 | D |
| D | 2–13 | 12 ASCII digits, payee account (retain leading zeros) |
| D | 14–25 | 12 ASCII digits, positive zero-padded integer minor units |
| D | 26–45 | 20 printable chars, nonblank reference, right-space padded |
| D | 46–80 | spaces |
| T | 1 | T |
| T | 2–7 | detail count, 6 ASCII digits |
| T | 8–22 | control total of amounts, 15 ASCII digits |
| T | 23–32 | hash total, 10 ASCII digits |
| T | 33–80 | spaces |

Exactly one header first, then one or more details, then exactly one trailer last. The count is
the number of D records, including malformed D records. Control total and hash total use only
fully valid detail records. Hash = sum(int(last 8 digits of each payee account)) mod 10**10.
Money is in header-currency minor units: CAD/USD/EUR/GBP=2 dp, JPY=0, KWD/BHD=3. Do not convert.
Returned references remove right padding only. Preserve detail order and account strings.

Any error rejects the whole batch: accepted=False and payments=[]. Report all detectable errors,
deduplicated by (line_no,code), sorted by line_no then code. Continue checking other records.
For a wrong-width or invalid-encoding record, report only that local error (no field errors),
but structural/order and trailer comparisons can still be reported. Field errors are one code per
record even if multiple fields are invalid. Trailer totals compare independently when its fields
are valid, even if other records failed.

Codes: invalid_encoding, line_length, record_type, record_order, invalid_header, invalid_detail,
invalid_trailer, count_mismatch, control_total_mismatch, hash_total_mismatch, missing_header,
missing_trailer. H outside first position, T outside last, or D at first/last -> record_order.
Unknown type -> record_type. A missing first H -> missing_header at line1; missing final T ->
missing_trailer at the last line (line1 for empty input). An empty detail section -> count_mismatch
on the trailer. Header/trailer field errors include invalid reserved spaces. No other codes.

Worked example (each record is padded with spaces to 80 characters; trailing spaces are not shown):

```text
HSYNTHBATCH0120260115CAD
D000000012345000000010000INV-2026-001
D000000067890000000002550INV-2026-002
T0000020000000000125500000080235
```

The batch is accepted with two payments: `Payment("000000012345", 10000, "INV-2026-001")` and
`Payment("000000067890", 2550, "INV-2026-002")`. Count 2; control total 10000 + 2550 = 12550; hash total
12345 + 67890 = 80235. Changing the trailer count to `000003` rejects the whole batch with
`BatchError(4, "count_mismatch")` and `payments == []`.

Run from starter: `python -m pytest tests -q`. No real batch format, institution or customer is used.
Reviewer notes (NOTES.md)
# Private draft - batch parser reviewer notes

v2 starter (see analysis/findings.md F-105): the starter interprets detail records only, splits the
file on LF only, never looks at the header or trailer, and returns the good payments even when other
detail lines were malformed. Header/trailer validation, count, control and hash totals, CRLF handling
and whole-batch rejection are the ticket. The wrong variants are plausible completions of that starter:
`wrong_partial_accept` adds everything but keeps accepting the valid details of a batch with
malformed detail records; `wrong_ignores_hash_total` implements count and control totals only;
`wrong_crlf_slicing` completes the validation but keeps the starter's LF-only line splitting, so CRLF
files fail on the CR byte. The alternative accumulates totals in a single streaming pass.
CR at the end does not shift earlier columns; the defect is validating/slicing before line-ending
normalization. Fixtures generate CRLF from bytes.

## Blind review
Author self-review: positions, terminators, malformed-field error granularity, cascading trailer
checks, ordering and line numbers all needed explicit rules. The ticket now contains them.
Predictions: account000000000123 contributes123 to hash; count mismatch -> rejected/no payments;
valid CRLF -> accepted/same records as LF. No unresolved graded ambiguity. Independent review owed.

Self-review confirmation (2026-10-17): re-read only PROBLEM.md and starter files from disk; the three predictions above follow from the ticket. No additional graded ambiguity found. Independent review remains owed.

+    if not physical or not physical[-1].startswith(b"T"):
+        issues.add((max(last_index, 1), "missing_trailer"))
+    for index, physical_record in enumerate(physical, start=1):
+        marker = physical_record[:1]
+        if marker == b"D":
+            number_of_details += 1
+        expected_position = {b"H": index == 1, b"T": index == last_index,
+                             b"D": 1 < index < last_index}
+        if marker in expected_position and not expected_position[marker]:
+            issues.add((index, "record_order"))
+        record, issue = decode(physical_record)
+        if issue is None:
+            value, issue = fields(record)
+            if issue is None and marker == b"D":
+                details.append(value)
+                amount_sum += value.amount_minor
+                account_sum = (account_sum + int(value.payee_account[-8:])) % 10**10
+            elif issue is None and marker == b"T":
+                controls.append((index, value))
+        if issue:
+            issues.add((index, issue))
+    for index, declared in controls:
+        observed = number_of_details, amount_sum, account_sum
+        codes = "count_mismatch", "control_total_mismatch", "hash_total_mismatch"
+        for i, code in enumerate(codes):
+            if declared[i] != observed[i] or (i == 0 and number_of_details == 0):
+                issues.add((index, code))
+    ordered_errors = [BatchError(line, code) for line, code in sorted(issues)]
+    if ordered_errors:
+        return BatchResult(False, [], ordered_errors)
+    return BatchResult(True, details, [])

Reproduce: python -m harness.run_all --grader all, then python -m harness.report --latest.

Limitations: the solutions are a finite, author-written sample, and same-host subprocess isolation is not a security boundary. Independent blind review, Docker timing and a live Harbor run are still outstanding.
